# Experiment Design: Osmotic and Macromolecular Crowding Gradients Discriminate Membrane-Bounded from Phase-Separated Compartmentalization via Cryo-Electron Tomography

## Selected Idea Direction
- **Central Hypothesis:** Under controlled hyperosmotic perturbation, membrane-bounded organelles resist internal densification and maintain sharp lipid-defined boundaries, whereas fluid-like phase-separated condensates undergo continuous density shifts and boundary coarsening; this distinction holds provided that global cytoskeletal integrity is maintained and non-specific macromolecular precipitation is excluded.
- **Id:** mechanism_replacement
- **Mechanism Or Relation:** Osmotic pressure gradients modulate macromolecular crowding and multivalent interactions to drive dispersed-to-aggregated transitions in phase-separated condensates, while lipid bilayer impermeability restricts membrane-bounded organelles to volume reduction without internal densification.
- **Title:** Osmotic and Macromolecular Crowding Gradients Discriminate Membrane-Bounded from Phase-Separated Compartmentalization via Cryo-Electron Tomography

## Research Scope
- **Alternative Explanations:**
  - Hyperosmotic shock induces non-specific protein aggregation or global cellular collapse rather than specific phase-separated condensate densification.
  - Membrane-bounded organelles may also exhibit internal density gradients or undergo structural changes that blur the distinction in boundary sharpness under extreme osmotic stress.
  - Global cytoskeletal collapse under hyperosmotic stress mechanically compresses membrane-bounded organelles, causing apparent boundary blurring and internal density shifts that mimic phase-separated condensate behavior.
  - Non-specific protein salting-out or precipitation at high osmolyte concentrations causes artificial aggregation and density gradients in all compartments, obscuring the specific phase-separation mechanism.
- **Boundary Conditions:**
  - The claim fails if membrane-bounded organelles exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion due to cytoskeletal compression, or if phase-separated condensates maintain sharp boundaries, or if non-specific salting-out produces identical density profiles across all compartments.
- **Discipline Ids:**
  - 24

  - **Mode:** DESIGN_ONLY
  - **Reason:** ExperimentDesign v1 is design-only and does not delegate work to a digital or physical executor.

    - **Design Implication:** Research designs involving macromolecular interactions should explicitly define whether the experimental or computational environment simulates molecular crowding, as results may differ significantly from dilute conditions.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific experimental protocols for inducing crowding.
      - The magnitude of effects on specific biological outcomes.

    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The statement is general and does not specify quantitative thresholds for crowding density.
      - It does not distinguish between different types of crowding agents (e.g., inert vs. interacting).

    - **Statement:** Macromolecules function in a crowded environment populated by numerous different molecules rather than acting in isolation.

    - **Card Id:** EC:W2522510678:boundary_conditions:3a915a792140
    - **Claim Slot:** boundary_conditions
    - **Design Implication:** When designing a study for physical science samples where single-particle averaging is proposed, one must verify that the sample consists of identical copies and address challenges related to diffraction and phase contrast in TEM images.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Comparison controls
      - Measurement calibration
      - Statistics bias
      - Risk ethics reproducibility
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** Although single-particle cryo–electron microscopy (cryo-EM) has been under rapid development for 3D structure determination of macromolecules with identical or similar conformations at near-atomic resolution, this method cannot be generally applied to the physical sciences for the following three reasons. First, most materials do not have identical copies and cannot be averaged to achieve atomic resolution. Second, a priori knowledge of the peptide sequence and stereochemistry in protein molecules greatly facilitates their 3D atomic structure determination, but this knowledge is not applicable to physical science samples. Third, unlike in biological specimens, the presence of diffraction and phase contrast in the transmission electron microscopy images of most materials poses a challenge for tomographic reconstruction.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The statement describes general challenges and reasons for limited applicability, not definitive impossibility for all physical science samples.
    - **Source Id:** W2522510678
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy is generally not applicable to physical science samples because materials lack identical copies for averaging, lack a priori knowledge of peptide sequences/stereochemistry, and exhibit diffraction and phase contrast that challenge tomographic reconstruction.

      - Causal conclusions beyond the scope of the summary
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** RNA binding-deficient TDP-43 (produced by neurodegeneration-causing mutations or posttranslational acetylation in its RNA recognition motifs) drove TDP-43 demixing into intranuclear liquid spherical shells with liquid cores. These droplets, which we named "anisosomes", have shells that exhibit birefringence, thus indicating liquid crystal formation. Guided by mathematical modeling, we identified the primary components of the liquid core to be HSP70 family chaperones, whose adenosine triphosphate (ATP)-dependent activity maintained the liquidity of shells and cores.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is an abstract-level claim and does not provide experimental details, controls, or statistical methods.

    - **Evidence Excerpt:** In addition, assembled tau associates with extracellular vesicles (EVs) in the central nervous system of patients with AD, which is linked to its clearance and prion-like propagation between neurons.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The excerpt states an association and a link to propagation/clearance but does not detail the specific molecular mechanisms of this link.
      - The text notes that the identities of the specific assembled tau species and EVs are not fully known.
    - **Source Id:** W4367556447
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Assembled tau associates with extracellular vesicles (EVs) in the central nervous system of patients with Alzheimer's disease, which is linked to its clearance and prion-like propagation between neurons.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4367556447:mechanism:f860357f0068
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** Experimental designs targeting EV-associated tau might consider the specific compartmentalization (endo-lysosomal) and structural interactions (membrane tethering) observed in AD brain EVs.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - The universality of this packaging mechanism across all tauopathies or non-human models.
      - The specific identity of all molecules involved in tethering.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** We found filaments of truncated tau enclosed within EVs enriched in endo-lysosomal proteins. We observed multiple filament interactions, including with molecules that tethered filaments to the EV limiting membrane, suggesting selective packaging.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This describes observations in brain EVs from AD patients studied by specific imaging/mass spectrometry methods.
      - The term 'suggesting selective packaging' indicates an inference from the observed interactions, not a definitive mechanism of sorting.
    - **Source Id:** W4367556447
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Filaments of truncated tau were found enclosed within EVs enriched in endo-lysosomal proteins, with evidence of multiple filament interactions including tethering to the EV limiting membrane.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4375948977:boundary_conditions:6e368b27ac6e
    - **Claim Slot:** boundary_conditions
    - **Design Implication:** If the research design requires structural data from macromolecular networks in a native cellular context, cryo-FIB-ET may be considered as a relevant modality.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific sample preparation requirements for native conditions
      - Resolution limits or success rates for macromolecular network identification
      - Comparison controls or specific statistical methods
      - Instrument calibration settings
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** the modality of cryo-electron tomography has fast developed into a bona fide in situ structural biology technique where structures are determined in their native environment, the cell
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This claim is derived from an abstract and describes general capabilities rather than specific experimental protocols.
      - The text does not define specific criteria for what constitutes 'native' versus 'altered' conditions.
    - **Source Id:** W4375948977
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Cryo-FIB-ET allows for the determination of structures within their native cellular environment, specifically in near-native conditions.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4387354254:mechanism:5942a26872f1
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If the research involves studying protein LLPS, experimental designs should consider measuring or controlling variables related to electrostatic, hydrophobic, and H-bonding interactions to assess their specific contributions to phase formation.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - The exact quantitative weights of each interaction type for every protein.
      - Generalizability to all proteins or polypeptides outside the studied set.
      - Specific experimental conditions or measurement techniques used to determine the role of these interactions.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** We further demonstrated that electrostatic, hydrophobic, and H-bonding or a combination of such intermolecular interactions plays a crucial role in individual protein/peptide LLPS.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The claim is limited to the specific proteins and polypeptides studied in this work (a total of 23).
      - As an abstract, it does not provide the quantitative data or detailed experimental methods used to distinguish the relative contributions of each interaction type.
    - **Source Id:** W4387354254
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Electrostatic, hydrophobic, and H-bonding interactions, or combinations thereof, are proposed to play a crucial role in the liquid-liquid phase separation (LLPS) of individual proteins and peptides.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4387354254:mechanism:c7bbe5f48f16
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** When selecting candidates for LLPS investigation, proteins or domains known to be intrinsically disordered or unstructured may be prioritized as they are hypothesized to facilitate the multivalent interactions necessary for phase separation.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - That all unstructured domains undergo LLPS.
      - The specific mechanism by which multivalent interactions occur in this study.
      - A causal link established by the experimental results of this specific paper for this general background fact.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** Unstructured and intrinsically disordered domains are known to facilitate multivalent interactions driving protein LLPS.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This statement reflects background knowledge or established context cited within the abstract rather than a new finding from this specific study.
      - It does not specify which types of unstructured domains were effective in this particular experiment.
    - **Source Id:** W4387354254
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Intrinsically disordered and unstructured domains are known to facilitate multivalent interactions that drive protein LLPS.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W2077202048:mechanism:0d46945291e3
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If studying high-Tc superconducting grain boundaries for application-oriented purposes, consider designs that explicitly account for their distinct electronic properties compared to conventional metallic superconductors.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific mathematical models or detailed physical mechanisms behind the unique electronic properties.
      - Quantitative data on how these properties affect device performance.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** Unique electronic properties, different from those of the grain boundaries in conventional metallic superconductors, have made grain boundaries formed by high-${T}_{\mathrm{c}}$ cuprates important tools for basic science. They are moreover a key issue for electronic and large-scale applications of high-${T}_{\mathrm{c}}$ superconductivity.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is an abstract-level statement and does not specify which unique electronic properties are relevant or how they manifest.
      - The excerpt describes general importance rather than a detailed physical mechanism.
    - **Source Id:** W2077202048
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Grain boundaries in high-Tc superconductors possess unique electronic properties that distinguish them from grain boundaries in conventional metallic superconductors, making them important for basic science and key issues for applications.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W3103021984:mechanism:7f612eec3fe3
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If investigating viral entry or assembly mechanisms, study designs should not assume S protein conformational changes alone drive membrane curvature, but should instead consider lateral reorganization during the assembly phase.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific experimental conditions used to observe the reorganization.
      - Quantitative metrics of membrane bending forces.
      - Causal links between other structural components and membrane bending.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** We report that assembled S trimers in lumenal cisternae do not alone induce membrane bending but laterally reorganize on the envelope during virion assembly.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - Abstract-level evidence only.
      - Does not specify the exact molecular triggers for the lateral reorganization.
    - **Source Id:** W3103021984
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Assembled S trimers in lumenal cisternae do not alone induce membrane bending but laterally reorganize on the envelope during virion assembly.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W3103021984:mechanism:26050f9e3e6a
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** Experimental setups aiming to capture vRNP recruitment should include observations of membrane curvature or budding sites, as recruitment appears associated with these geometric features.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Statistical significance of the accumulation at curved membranes.
      - Specific instrument settings for detecting vRNPs.
      - Whether this mechanism applies universally to all coronaviruses.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** The viral ribonucleoprotein complexes (vRNPs) are accumulated at the curved membrane characteristic for budding sites suggesting that vRNP recruitment is enhanced by membrane curvature.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The statement of enhancement is described as a suggestion in the source text.
      - Abstract-level evidence only.
    - **Source Id:** W3103021984
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Viral ribonucleoprotein complexes (vRNPs) are accumulated at curved membranes characteristic of budding sites, suggesting membrane curvature enhances vRNP recruitment.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W3103021984:mechanism:5998bf8e54cc
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** Models of SARS-CoV-2 genome packaging should consider multiple discrete vRNP units rather than a single continuous complex to account for the large genome size and spatial constraints.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Exact count of vRNP complexes per virion.
      - Detailed structural parameters of the cylindrical assemblies.
      - Experimental validation of the 'steric flexibility' claim beyond the proposal.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** We propose that the genome is packaged around multiple separate vRNP complexes, thereby allowing incorporation of the unusually large coronavirus genome into the virion while maintaining high steric flexibility between the vRNPs.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is stated as a proposal in the text.
      - Abstract-level evidence only.
    - **Source Id:** W3103021984
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** The SARS-CoV-2 genome is proposed to be packaged around multiple separate cylindrical vRNP complexes, allowing incorporation of the large genome while maintaining steric flexibility.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W3165280603:mechanism:315e8e621a45
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If the research design targets the mechanism of SARS-CoV-2 replication or spread, consider assays that capture changes in the cellular RNA-bound proteome or RNA metabolic pathways.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific experimental protocols or instrument settings.
      - Statistical methods used to determine significance.
      - Causal conclusions about specific individual proteins.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** We reveal that SARS-CoV-2 infection profoundly remodels the cellular RNA-bound proteome, which includes wide-ranging effects on RNA metabolic pathways, non-canonical RBPs, and antiviral factors.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is an abstract-level claim.
      - The specific extent and clinical significance of the 'profound' remodeling are not defined in the text.
    - **Source Id:** W3165280603
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** SARS-CoV-2 infection remodels the cellular RNA-bound proteome, including effects on RNA metabolic pathways, non-canonical RBPs, and antiviral factors.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W3165280603:mechanism:4446cc1ee94c
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If the research design involves host factors interacting with viral RNA, consider including tRNA ligase complex components as potential targets for functional validation.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Eligibility criteria for host cells.
      - Specific controls used in the regulatory assessment.
      - Reproducibility metrics.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** Among them are several components of the tRNA ligase complex, which we show regulate SARS-CoV-2 infection.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is an abstract-level claim.
      - The specific components of the tRNA ligase complex are not listed.
      - The direction or magnitude of regulation is not specified.
    - **Source Id:** W3165280603
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Components of the tRNA ligase complex are identified as regulators of SARS-CoV-2 infection.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4310332162:mechanism:9884c8df2c0c
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** A study design aiming to capture phagophore formation mechanisms should consider targeting conditions analogous to starvation and may need to account for ER association as a baseline structural feature of the phagophore.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Causal mechanisms driving ER association.
      - Generalizability to non-starvation conditions.
      - Specific molecular mediators of the contact sites.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** As previously reported, all phagophores associate with the ER. The surface area of phagophores in contact with the ER decreases gradually as they mature into autophagosomes and autolysosomes. However, the ER still associates with 92% of autophagosomes and 79% of autolysosomes, suggesting that most autophagosomes remain on the ER after closure and even when they fuse with lysosomes.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - Findings are based on abstract-level reporting.
      - The observation is specific to the reported experimental condition (cells starved 30 min).
    - **Source Id:** W4310332162
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** The supplied text reports that all phagophores associate with the endoplasmic reticulum (ER) in cells starved for 30 minutes, and suggests that most autophagosomes remain on the ER after closure.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4311695710:mechanism:7ce8f5c3377a
    - **Claim Slot:** mechanism
    - **Design Implication:** If proteomic data is prioritized over transcriptomic data for determining salt tolerance mechanisms, the study design must include protein extraction and analysis workflows rather than relying solely on RNA sequencing.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Causal mechanisms for specific proteins.
      - Quantitative differences in reliability between protein and gene expression data.
      - Experimental protocols for protein analysis.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** proteins are a more reliable determinant of salt tolerance than simple gene expression as they play major roles in shaping physiological traits in salt-tolerant phenotypes.
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is a general theoretical statement within a review abstract, not an experimental result.
      - It does not specify which proteins or pathways are responsible.
    - **Source Id:** W4311695710
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Proteins are considered more reliable determinants of salt tolerance than gene expression because they shape physiological traits in salt-tolerant phenotypes.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4414081957:study_design:2ff21048fef4
    - **Claim Slot:** study_design
    - **Design Implication:** If adopting the described quantitative analysis framework, design the sampling strategy to include systematic random sampling (SUR) and integrate deep learning algorithms like Gold Digger.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Statistical power calculations.
      - Specific bias correction methods.
      - Experimental validation of the framework's accuracy.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** It also introduces a quantitative analysis framework based on systematic random sampling (SUR) and deep learning algorithms (such as Gold Digger)
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - This is an abstract-level statement describing the framework introduced in the article.
      - It does not provide specific parameters, thresholds, or validation data for the SUR or deep learning components.
    - **Source Id:** W4414081957
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** The Immunoelectron Microscopy (IEM) workflow includes a quantitative analysis framework based on systematic random sampling (SUR) and deep learning algorithms such as Gold Digger.
  -
    - **Card Id:** EC:W4414081957:boundary_conditions:32ac6a2553e1
    - **Claim Slot:** boundary_conditions
    - **Design Implication:** When selecting post-embedding labeling for applications requiring ultrastructural integrity, plan experiments to address potential trade-offs between resin penetration and antigenic epitope masking.
    - **Does Not Establish:**
      - Specific chemical concentrations or incubation times to resolve these trade-offs.
      - Quantitative metrics for 'balance' between penetration and masking.
    - **Evidence Excerpt:** the latter has better tissue structure preservation but needs to balance the problems of resin penetration and antigenic epitope masking
    - **Evidence Level:** abstract
    - **Limitations:**
      - The abstract mentions the need to balance these factors but does not specify which factors are dominant or how to quantitatively resolve them.
      - Specific protocols for balancing these issues are not detailed in the abstract.
    - **Source Id:** W4414081957
    - **Source Location:** abstract:openalex
    - **Statement:** Post-embedding labeling in IEM maintains ultrastructural integrity but requires balancing resin penetration and antigenic epitope masking.

        - **Schema Version:** experiment_design_paper_screen_v1
        - **Slot Assessments:**
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** changing the atom-atom interaction from repulsive to attractive using a Feshbach resonance
            - **Rationale:** The abstract explicitly describes the physical mechanism (Feshbach resonance changing interaction sign) used to induce the collapse.
            - **Relation:** direct_support
            - **Slot:** mechanism
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** measuring the onset of this transition as a function of number and attractive interaction strength
            - **Rationale:** The text confirms that the key variables (onset of transition, atom number, interaction strength) were measured.
            - **Relation:** direct_support
            - **Slot:** research_object_measurability
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** Stable 85Rb BECs were created and then caused to collapse
            - **Rationale:** The abstract outlines the experimental procedure of creating a stable condensate and then inducing collapse, defining the study design.
            - **Relation:** direct_support
            - **Slot:** study_design
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** Stable 85Rb BECs were created
            - **Rationale:** The creation of a 'Stable' state serves as the baseline or control condition against which the 'collapse' is compared, though a separate control group is not explicitly detailed.
            - **Relation:** limited_support
            - **Slot:** comparison_controls
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** Feshbach resonance
            - **Rationale:** The use of a Feshbach resonance implies calibration of interaction strength, but the abstract does not provide specific details on the calibration procedure itself.
            - **Relation:** background
            - **Slot:** measurement_calibration
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** N(absolute value of a) / a(ho) = 0.459+/-0.012+/-0.054
            - **Rationale:** The abstract provides the determined stability condition with two distinct uncertainty terms (statistical and systematic errors), which is essential for assessing bias.
            - **Relation:** direct_support
            - **Slot:** statistics_bias
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              -
                - **Source:** abstract
                - **Text:** At a critical value, an abrupt transition was observed
            - **Rationale:** The text explicitly identifies the critical value where the behavior changes from stable to collapsing, defining the boundary condition.
            - **Relation:** direct_support
            - **Slot:** boundary_conditions
          -
            - **Evidence Anchors:**
              - not provided
            - **Rationale:** The supplied title and abstract contain no information regarding risks, ethical issues, or reproducibility of the experiment.

  - **Branch Id:** supports_mechanism

  - **Evidence Status:** EXPECTED_NOT_OBSERVED
  - **Improvement Actions:**
    - Replicate under independently confirmed conditions.
    - Test the most consequential declared boundary condition.
  - **Interpretation:** The result would support, but not prove, the declared relation within the design boundary.
  - **Schema Version:** outcome_branch_v1
  - **Trigger:** The prespecified analysis is consistent with the declared relation while planned controls do not favor a stated alternative explanation.
-
  - **Branch Id:** partial_or_heterogeneous
  - **Conclusion Scope:** The claim fails if membrane-bounded organelles exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion due to cytoskeletal compression, or if phase-separated condensates maintain sharp boundaries, or if non-specific salting-out produces identical density profiles across all compartments.
  - **Evidence Status:** EXPECTED_NOT_OBSERVED
  - **Improvement Actions:**

    - Improve the coverage of conditions and measurement comparability.
  - **Interpretation:** The relation may be conditional or heterogeneous; no universal conclusion is warranted.
  - **Schema Version:** outcome_branch_v1
  - **Trigger:** The prespecified analysis indicates variation across declared conditions, units, or measurement contexts.
-
  - **Branch Id:** null_or_contradictory
  - **Conclusion Scope:** The claim fails if membrane-bounded organelles exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion due to cytoskeletal compression, or if phase-separated condensates maintain sharp boundaries, or if non-specific salting-out produces identical density profiles across all compartments.
  - **Evidence Status:** EXPECTED_NOT_OBSERVED
  - **Improvement Actions:**

  - **Interpretation:** The proposed relation is not supported in this design boundary; absence of support is not proof of absence generally.

-

  - **Conclusion Scope:** The claim fails if membrane-bounded organelles exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion due to cytoskeletal compression, or if phase-separated condensates maintain sharp boundaries, or if non-specific salting-out produces identical density profiles across all compartments.

          "Risk ethics reproducibility"
        ],
        "evidence_excerpt": "Although single-particle cryo–electron microscopy (cryo-EM) has been under rapid development for 3D structure determination of macromolecules with identical or similar conformations at near-atomic resolution, this method cannot be generally applied to the physical sciences for the following three reasons. First, most materials do not have identical copies and cannot be averaged to achieve atomic resolution. Second, a priori knowledge of the peptide sequence and stereochemistry in protein molecules greatly facilitates their 3D atomic structure determination, but this knowledge is not applicable to physical science samples. Third, unlike in biological specimens, the presence of diffraction and phase contrast in the transmission electron microscopy images of most materials poses a challenge for tomographic reconstruction.",
        "evidence_level": "abstract",
        "limitations": [
          "The statement describes general challenges and reasons for limited applicability, not definitive impossibility for all physical science samples."
        ],
        "source_id": "W2522510678",
        "source_location": "abstract:openalex",
        "statement": "Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy is generally not applicable to physical science samples because materials lack identical copies for averaging, lack a priori knowledge of peptide sequences/stereochemistry, and exhibit diffraction and phase contrast that challenge tomographic reconstruction."
      },
      {
        "card_id": "EC:W2972404545:research_object_measurability:2d2b311811ce",
        "claim_slot": "research_object_measurability",
        "design_implication": "If measuring single-organelle stoichiometry is a design goal, consider whether bulk population proteomics scaled by population counts is an acceptable proxy for single-entity analysis, or if direct single-entity instrumentation is required.",
        "does_not_establish": [
          "Specific instrument settings or calibration standards used for intensity-based absolute quantification.",
          "The exact mathematical criteria used to scale population data to single-organelle values."
        ],
        "evidence_excerpt": "We perform proteomics on purified mitochondria from cultured heterotrophic Arabidopsis cells with intensity-based absolute quantification and scale the dataset to the single organelle based on criteria that are justified by experimental evidence and theoretical considerations.",
        "evidence_level": "abstract",
        "limitations": [
          "Abstract-level description only; specific scaling criteria and calibration methods are not defined in the text.",
          "The claim refers to an 'average' organelle, not necessarily every individual organelle's unique state."
        ],
        "source_id": "W2972404545",
        "source_location": "abstract:openalex",
        "statement": "The protein composition of an individual average plant mitochondrion was estimated via proteomics of purified mitochondria from cultured heterotrophic Arabidopsis cells, scaled to the single organelle."
      },
      {
        "card_id": "EC:W3112951837:mechanism:2e1a0f333c5b",
        "claim_slot": "mechanism",
        "design_implication": "If modeling TDP-43 phase separation, consider including HSP70 chaperones and ATP availability as key regulators of shell and core liquidity.",
        "does_not_establish": [
          "The specific quantitative parameters of HSP70 activity",
          "The detailed experimental setup or sample sizes",
          "Causal conclusions beyond the scope of the summary"
        ],
        "evidence_excerpt": "RNA binding-deficient TDP-43 (produced by neurodegeneration-causing mutations or posttranslational acetylation in its RNA recognition motifs) drove TDP-43 demixing into intranuclear liquid spherical shells with liquid cores. These droplets, which we named \"anisosomes\", have shells that exhibit birefringence, thus indicating liquid crystal formation. Guided by mathematical modeling, we identified the primary components of the liquid core to be HSP70 family chaperones, whose adenosine triphosphate (ATP)-dependent activity maintained the liquidity of shells and cores.",
        "evidence_level": "abstract",
        "limitations": [
          "This is an abstract-level claim and does not provide experimental details, controls, or statistical methods."
        ],
        "source_id": "W3112951837",
        "source_location": "abstract:openalex",
        "statement": "RNA binding-deficient TDP-43 drives demixing into intranuclear liquid spherical shells with liquid cores, termed 'anisosomes', whose liquidity is maintained by ATP-dependent HSP70 chaperone activity."
      },
      {
        "card_id": "EC:W3112951837:mechanism:36de7e278c93",
        "claim_slot": "mechanism",
        "design_implication": "In experimental designs, manipulate ATP levels to observe transitions from anisosomes to aggregates if studying phase conversion.",
        "does_not_establish": [

          "The specific assays used to measure aggregation"

        "evidence_excerpt": "These structures converted to aggregates when ATP levels were reduced.",
        "evidence_level": "abstract",
        "limitations": [

        ],
        "source_id": "W3112951837",
        "source_location": "abstract:openalex",
        "statement": "ATP reduction leads to the conversion of TDP-43-containing anisosomes into aggregates."
      },
      {
        "card_id": "EC:W3112951837:boundary_conditions:9fc40acebcba",

      },
      {

        "claim_slot": "mechanism",
        "design_implication": "If investigating EV-mediated tau pathology, designs may consider the dual role of EVs in both tau clearance and propagation.",

        "design_implication": "Experimental designs targeting EV-associated tau might consider the specific compartmentalization (endo-lysosomal) and structural interactions (membrane tethering) observed in AD brain EVs.",

        "claim_slot": "mechanism",
        "design_implication": "If the research involves studying protein LLPS, experimental designs should consider measuring or controlling variables related to electrostatic, hydrophobic, and H-bonding interactions to assess their specific contributions to phase formation.",

              {
                "evidence_anchors": [
                  {
                    "source": "abstract",
                    "text": "prebiotic chemical evolution started in microspaces with membranous walls, where external temperature and osmotic gradients were coupled to free-energy gradients of potential chemical reactions"
                  },
                  {
                    "source": "abstract",
                    "text": "Small ions and ionic metabolites are transported vectorially between the electrolyte pools and through the (semiconducting) electrolyte pathways of the crowded macromolecular regions from their high electrochemical potential (where they are biochemically produced) to their lower electrochemical potential (where they are consumed)"
                  }
                ],
                "rationale": "The abstract explicitly describes the mechanistic link between physicochemical gradients and vectorial transport within microspaces.",
                "relation": "direct_support",
                "slot": "mechanism"
              },
              {
                "evidence_anchors": [
                  {
                    "source": "abstract",
                    "text": "average macromolecular crowding of 20 to 30% of the cell volume"
                  },
                  {
                    "source": "abstract",
                    "text": "high ionic strength of the Hadean ocean (short Debye's lengths)"
                  }
                ],
                "rationale": "The text mentions quantitative thresholds (crowding percentage) and measurable physical parameters (ionic strength, Debye lengths) relevant to the research object, though specific measurement instruments are not detailed.",
                "relation": "limited_support",
                "slot": "research_object_measurability"
              },
              {
                "evidence_anchors": [
                  {
                    "source": "abstract",
                    "text": "We have developed a general scenario of prebiotic physicochemical evolution during the Earth's Hadean eon and reviewed the relevant literature"

                ],
                "rationale": "The design is a theoretical model/scenario review; this is contextual information about the study type but does not support a specific experimental design method.",

  "research_brief": {
    "alternative_explanations": [
      "Hyperosmotic shock induces non-specific protein aggregation or global cellular collapse rather than specific phase-separated condensate densification.",
      "Membrane-bounded organelles may also exhibit internal density gradients or undergo structural changes that blur the distinction in boundary sharpness under extreme osmotic stress.",
      "Global cytoskeletal collapse under hyperosmotic stress mechanically compresses membrane-bounded organelles, causing apparent boundary blurring and internal density shifts that mimic phase-separated condensate behavior.",
      "Non-specific protein salting-out or precipitation at high osmolyte concentrations causes artificial aggregation and density gradients in all compartments, obscuring the specific phase-separation mechanism."
    ],
    "boundary_conditions": [
      "The claim fails if membrane-bounded organelles exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion due to cytoskeletal compression, or if phase-separated condensates maintain sharp boundaries, or if non-specific salting-out produces identical density profiles across all compartments."
    ],
    "brief_id": "20260901-042802-922870",
    "discipline_ids": [
      "24"
    ],
    "discriminating_observations": [
      "Differential boundary sharpness and internal spatial density profiles measured via cryo_tomography_assay: condensates exhibit continuous density gradients and fusion, while membrane-bounded organelles exhibit sharp density discontinuities and volume reduction without fusion, under conditions where cytoskeletal artifacts are controlled."
    ],
    "evidence_status": "PROPOSED",

    "research_object": {
      "description": "cellular_biomolecular_system_comprising_membrane_bounded_organelles_and_phase_separated_condensates"
    },
    "schema_version": "research_brief_v1",
    "selected_direction": {
      "central_hypothesis": "Under controlled hyperosmotic perturbation, membrane-bounded organelles resist internal densification and maintain sharp lipid-defined boundaries, whereas fluid-like phase-separated condensates undergo continuous density shifts and boundary coarsening; this distinction holds provided that global cytoskeletal integrity is maintained and non-specific macromolecular precipitation is excluded.",
      "id": "mechanism_replacement",
      "mechanism_or_relation": "Osmotic pressure gradients modulate macromolecular crowding and multivalent interactions to drive dispersed-to-aggregated transitions in phase-separated condensates, while lipid bilayer impermeability restricts membrane-bounded organelles to volume reduction without internal densification.",
      "title": "Osmotic and Macromolecular Crowding Gradients Discriminate Membrane-Bounded from Phase-Separated Compartmentalization via Cryo-Electron Tomography"
    },

    "topic": "How are biomolecules organized in cells to function orderly and effectively? This project investigates the multiscale principles that organize proteins, nucleic acids, lipids, and carbohydrates in crowded cells. It focuses on the complementary roles of membrane-bounded organelles and biomolecular phase separation in controlling molecular localization, condensation, availability, and functional coordination. The study will synthesize mechanistic evidence from cryo-fluorescence microscopy and cryo-electron tomography to formulate testable, design-only research strategies for resolving nanoscale biomolecular organization across cellular phases. The workflow must remain computational and evidence-driven, with any proposed biological experiments explicitly marked NEED_HUMAN_INPUT for specialist feasibility and ethics review."
  },
